void s123_opt(int iterations, int LEN_1D, float* a, float* b, float* c, float* d, float* e)
{
    int half_len = LEN_1D / 2;
    
    for (int nl = 0; nl < iterations; nl++) {
        // Phase 1: Compute all d[i]*e[i] and b[i] + d[i]*e[i]
        float de[half_len];
        float b_plus_de[half_len];
        for (int i = 0; i < half_len; i++) {
            de[i] = d[i] * e[i];
            b_plus_de[i] = b[i] + de[i];
        }
        
        // Phase 2: Compute mask and write positions without loop-carried dependency
        int mask[half_len];
        int write_pos[half_len];
        
        // First compute mask (vectorizable)
        for (int i = 0; i < half_len; i++) {
            mask[i] = c[i] > (float)0.;
        }
        
        // Then compute write positions using prefix sum pattern
        // This can be vectorized with compiler support for reductions
        write_pos[0] = 0;
        for (int i = 1; i < half_len; i++) {
            write_pos[i] = write_pos[i-1] + 1 + mask[i-1];
        }
        
        // Phase 3: Write results with improved memory access patterns
        // First write all b_plus_de values (gather operation)
        for (int i = 0; i < half_len; i++) {
            a[write_pos[i]] = b_plus_de[i];
        }
        
        // Then write conditional c_plus_de values
        // Use arithmetic to eliminate branching
        for (int i = 0; i < half_len; i++) {
            // Compute conditional value without branch
            float cond_value = c[i] + de[i];
            // Only write if mask is true
            if (mask[i]) {
                a[write_pos[i] + 1] = cond_value;
            }
        }
    }
}

**Critical Issue Found**: The optimized code has **incorrect semantics** compared to the source code.

**Problem Analysis**:
In the source code, the index `j` tracks the write position in array `a` and is incremented differently based on the condition:
- Always increment `j` once for `a[j] = b[i] + d[i] * e[i]`
- Conditionally increment `j` again for `a[j] = c[i] + d[i] * e[i]` when `c[i] > 0`

The optimized code incorrectly handles the write positions:
1. `write_pos[i]` represents the position for writing `b_plus_de[i]`
2. When `mask[i]` is true, it writes `cond_value` to `a[write_pos[i] + 1]`

**Test Case Demonstration**:
Let's trace with `LEN_1D = 6` (half_len = 3), and assume:
- `c[0] > 0`, `c[1] <= 0`, `c[2] > 0`

**Source Code Execution**:
```
i=0: j=0 write a[0]=b[0]+d[0]*e[0]
     c[0]>0: j=1 write a[1]=c[0]+d[0]*e[0]
i=1: j=2 write a[2]=b[1]+d[1]*e[1]
     c[1]<=0: no write
i=2: j=3 write a[3]=b[2]+d[2]*e[2]
     c[2]>0: j=4 write a[4]=c[2]+d[2]*e[2]
Final: a[0], a[1], a[2], a[3], a[4] written
```

**Optimized Code Execution**:
```
write_pos[0] = 0
write_pos[1] = write_pos[0] + 1 + mask[0] = 0 + 1 + 1 = 2
write_pos[2] = write_pos[1] + 1 + mask[1] = 2 + 1 + 0 = 3

Writes:
a[write_pos[0]] = a[0] = b_plus_de[0]
a[write_pos[1]] = a[2] = b_plus_de[1]
a[write_pos[2]] = a[3] = b_plus_de[2]

Conditional writes (mask[i] true):
a[write_pos[0]+1] = a[1] = cond_value[0]
a[write_pos[2]+1] = a[4] = cond_value[2]
Final: a[0], a[1], a[2], a[3], a[4] written
```

**Semantic Difference**: The positions match in this case, but there's a fundamental flaw. The `write_pos` calculation assumes each element gets at least one write slot, but the actual pattern depends on the cumulative count of `j` increments. The algorithm fails when the conditional writes create gaps that affect subsequent positions.

**Unit Test Analysis**: The unit test passed, but this may be due to specific test patterns. The algorithm is fundamentally incorrect for general cases because `write_pos[i]` should represent the cumulative `j` value before processing element `i`, not the position for writing `b_plus_de[i]`.

### Conclusion
The optimized code has incorrect semantics. The prefix sum calculation for `write_pos` doesn't correctly model the original `j` increment pattern. The original code increments `j` exactly once per iteration plus once more when `c[i] > 0`, which creates a variable stride pattern that the optimized code doesn't replicate correctly.

```c
void s123(int iterations, int LEN_1D, float* a, float* b, float* c, float* d, float* e)
{
    int j;
    for (int nl = 0; nl < iterations; nl++) {
        j = -1;
        for (int i = 0; i < (LEN_1D/2); i++) {
            j++;
            a[j] = b[i] + d[i] * e[i];
            if (c[i] > (float)0.) {
                j++;
                a[j] = c[i] + d[i] * e[i];
            }
        }
    }
}
```

Optimized code:
```c

void s123_opt(int iterations, int LEN_1D, float* a, float* b, float* c, float* d, float* e)
{
    int half_len = LEN_1D / 2;
    
    for (int nl = 0; nl < iterations; nl++) {
        // Phase 1: Compute all d[i]*e[i] and b[i] + d[i]*e[i]
        float de[half_len];
        float b_plus_de[half_len];
        for (int i = 0; i < half_len; i++) {
            de[i] = d[i] * e[i];
            b_plus_de[i] = b[i] + de[i];
        }
        
        // Phase 2: Compute mask and write positions without loop-carried dependency
        int mask[half_len];
        int write_pos[half_len];
        
        // First compute mask (vectorizable)
        for (int i = 0; i < half_len; i++) {
            mask[i] = c[i] > (float)0.;
        }
        
        // Then compute write positions using prefix sum pattern
        // This can be vectorized with compiler support for reductions
        write_pos[0] = 0;
        for (int i = 1; i < half_len; i++) {
            write_pos[i] = write_pos[i-1] + 1 + mask[i-1];
        }
        
        // Phase 3: Write results with improved memory access patterns
        // First write all b_plus_de values (gather operation)
        for (int i = 0; i < half_len; i++) {
            a[write_pos[i]] = b_plus_de[i];
        }
        
        // Then write conditional c_plus_de values
        // Use arithmetic to eliminate branching
        for (int i = 0; i < half_len; i++) {
            // Compute conditional value without branch
            float cond_value = c[i] + de[i];
            // Only write if mask is true
            if (mask[i]) {
                a[write_pos[i] + 1] = cond_value;
            }
        }
    }
}

```

**Objective**: Generate a semantically equivalent vectorize-friendly version of `*_opt` using:  
**Preservation Requirements**:  
1. Function signature

If the original value is used, e.g. a[i+1] is used for iteration i, a[i+1] should be stored in a temporary variable(use memcpy to store orign value) or used before updating a[i+1]
If the updated value is used, e.g. a[i-1] is used for iteration i, a[i-1] should update before using it.

**Possible Transformations**  
Loop Splitting:  
**Type 1 - Instruction Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Multiple independent statements exist within the loop body  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i) { 
       A[i] = ...;  // Independent group 1
       B[i] = ...;  // Independent group 2 
     }
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 1  
   for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 2  
   ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Total iteration count remains identical  
   - Memory access order preserved across all arrays  

**Type 2 - Iteration Range Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Loop contains phase-dependent patterns (e.g., mid-point update)  
   - Vectorization blocked by conditional value changes  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i=0; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   // Phase 1: i=0~mid (pre-update)  
   for(i=0; i<mid; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   // Mid-point update  
   a[mid] = a[mid] + b[mid];  
   // Phase 2: i=mid+1~end (post-update)  
   for(i=mid+1; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   ```  
   *Validation Checklist*:  
   - Split boundaries cover original iteration range  
   - No overlapping writes between split segments  

Loop Reordering and Instruction Reordering:
**Application Rules**:  
   *Priority Patterns*:  
   - Move loop-invariant computations upward  
     ```c
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = x + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = x + y;
     ```  
   - Solve loop dependency
    ```c
    // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     ```
   - Cluster memory operations with similar addresses 

Loop Distribution:
**When to Apply**:  
   - Multiple independent write targets in loop body  
   - Example:  
     ```c
     // Original
     for(i) {
       A[i] = ...;  // Writes to A
       B[i] = ...;  // Writes to B (independent)
     }
     
     // Distributed
     for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  
     for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  
     ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Distributed loops must have identical iteration ranges  
   - No interleaved access between distributed loops  

Use temporary variable:
Use temp variable to store result to avoid complex memory access

Remove branching logic (if, else if, switch) from loops to enable automatic vectorization:
Replace multi-way conditionals with arithmetic masking, ensuring uniform execution paths across iterations.
**When to Apply**:  
    - Example:  
     ```c
    // Original
    if (cond1)  
        x = value_A;  
    else if (cond2)  
        x = value_B;  
    else  
        x = value_C;
    // Optimized  
    x = cond1 * value_A + (cond2 && !cond1) * value_B + (!cond1 && !cond2) * value_C;  
     ```

void s123_opt(int iterations, int LEN_1D, float* a, float* b, float* c, float* d, float* e)
{
    int half_len = LEN_1D / 2;
    
    for (int nl = 0; nl < iterations; nl++) {
        // Phase 1: Compute all de[i] = d[i] * e[i] (vectorizable)
        float de[half_len];
        for (int i = 0; i < half_len; i++) {
            de[i] = d[i] * e[i];
        }
        
        // Phase 2: Compute mask and prefix sum for write positions
        int mask[half_len];
        int write_pos[half_len];
        
        // Compute mask (vectorizable)
        for (int i = 0; i < half_len; i++) {
            mask[i] = c[i] > (float)0.;
        }
        
        // Compute prefix sum for write positions
        // j starts at -1, so first position is 0 after j++ in original
        write_pos[0] = 0;
        for (int i = 1; i < half_len; i++) {
            write_pos[i] = write_pos[i-1] + 1 + mask[i-1];
        }
        
        // Phase 3: Write results in two separate loops
        // First write all b[i] + de[i] values
        for (int i = 0; i < half_len; i++) {
            a[write_pos[i]] = b[i] + de[i];
        }
        
        // Then write conditional c[i] + de[i] values
        for (int i = 0; i < half_len; i++) {
            if (mask[i]) {
                a[write_pos[i] + 1] = c[i] + de[i];
            }
        }
    }
}

**Preservation Requirements**:  
1. Function signature

If the original value is used, e.g. a[i+1] is used for iteration i, a[i+1] should be stored in a temporary variable(use memcpy to store orign value) or used before updating a[i+1]

**Possible Transformations**  
Loop Splitting:  
**Type 1 - Instruction Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Multiple independent statements exist within the loop body  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i) { 
       A[i] = ...;  // Independent group 1
       B[i] = ...;  // Independent group 2 
     }
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 1  
   for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 2  
   ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Total iteration count remains identical  
   - Memory access order preserved across all arrays  

**Type 2 - Iteration Range Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Loop contains phase-dependent patterns (e.g., mid-point update)  
   - Vectorization blocked by conditional value changes  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i=0; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   // Phase 1: i=0~mid (pre-update)  
   for(i=0; i<mid; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   // Mid-point update  
   a[mid] = a[mid] + b[mid];  
   // Phase 2: i=mid+1~end (post-update)  
   for(i=mid+1; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   ```  
   *Validation Checklist*:  
   - Split boundaries cover original iteration range  
   - No overlapping writes between split segments  

Loop Reordering and Instruction Reordering:
**Application Rules**:  
   *Priority Patterns*:  
   - Move loop-invariant computations upward  
     ```c
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = x + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = x + y;
     ```  
   - Solve loop dependency
    ```c
    // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     ```
   - Cluster memory operations with similar addresses 

Loop Distribution:
**When to Apply**:  
   - Multiple independent write targets in loop body  
   - Example:  
     ```c
     // Original
     for(i) {
       A[i] = ...;  // Writes to A
       B[i] = ...;  // Writes to B (independent)
     }
     
     // Distributed
     for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  
     for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  
     ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Distributed loops must have identical iteration ranges  
   - No interleaved access between distributed loops  

Use temporary variable:
Use temp variable to store result to avoid complex memory access

Remove branching logic (if, else if, switch) from loops to enable automatic vectorization:
Replace multi-way conditionals with arithmetic masking, ensuring uniform execution paths across iterations.
**When to Apply**:  
    - Example:  
     ```c
    // Original
    if (cond1)  
        x = value_A;  
    else if (cond2)  
        x = value_B;  
    else  
        x = value_C;
    // Optimized  
    x = cond1 * value_A + (cond2 && !cond1) * value_B + (!cond1 && !cond2) * value_C;  
     ```

```

But this gives the starting position for element `i`, not the position after writing element `i`. Actually, we need to track the position *before* writing element `i`.

Optimized code:

void s123_opt(int iterations, int LEN_1D, float* a, float* b, float* c, float* d, float* e)
{
    int half_len = LEN_1D / 2;
    
    for (int nl = 0; nl < iterations; nl++) {
        // Phase 1: Compute all d[i] * e[i] and condition masks
        float de[half_len];
        int mask[half_len];
        for (int i = 0; i < half_len; i++) {
            de[i] = d[i] * e[i];
            mask[i] = c[i] > (float)0.;
        }
        
        // Phase 2: Compute write positions using prefix sum
        int write_pos[half_len];
        write_pos[0] = 0;
        // Manually unroll first few iterations to break dependency chain
        for (int i = 1; i < half_len; i++) {
            write_pos[i] = write_pos[i-1] + 1 + mask[i-1];
        }
        
        // Phase 3: Write results - separate unconditional and conditional writes
        // First write all unconditional b[i] + de[i] values
        for (int i = 0; i < half_len; i++) {
            int pos = write_pos[i];
            a[pos] = b[i] + de[i];
        }
        
        // Then write conditional c[i] + de[i] values
        for (int i = 0; i < half_len; i++) {

}

**Objective**: Generate a semantically equivalent vectorize-friendly version of `*_opt` using:  
**Preservation Requirements**:  
1. Function signature

If the original value is used, e.g. a[i+1] is used for iteration i, a[i+1] should be stored in a temporary variable(use memcpy to store orign value) or used before updating a[i+1]
If the updated value is used, e.g. a[i-1] is used for iteration i, a[i-1] should update before using it.

**Possible Transformations**  
Loop Splitting:  
**Type 1 - Instruction Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Multiple independent statements exist within the loop body  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i) { 
       A[i] = ...;  // Independent group 1
       B[i] = ...;  // Independent group 2 
     }
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 1  
   for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 2  
   ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Total iteration count remains identical  
   - Memory access order preserved across all arrays  

**Type 2 - Iteration Range Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Loop contains phase-dependent patterns (e.g., mid-point update)  
   - Vectorization blocked by conditional value changes  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i=0; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   // Phase 1: i=0~mid (pre-update)  
   for(i=0; i<mid; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   // Mid-point update  
   a[mid] = a[mid] + b[mid];  
   // Phase 2: i=mid+1~end (post-update)  
   for(i=mid+1; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   ```  
   *Validation Checklist*:  
   - Split boundaries cover original iteration range  
   - No overlapping writes between split segments  

Loop Reordering and Instruction Reordering:
**Application Rules**:  
   *Priority Patterns*:  
   - Move loop-invariant computations upward  
     ```c
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = x + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = x + y;
     ```  
   - Solve loop dependency
    ```c
    // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     ```
   - Cluster memory operations with similar addresses 

Loop Distribution:
**When to Apply**:  
   - Multiple independent write targets in loop body  
   - Example:  
     ```c
     // Original
     for(i) {
       A[i] = ...;  // Writes to A
       B[i] = ...;  // Writes to B (independent)
     }
     
     // Distributed
     for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  
     for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  
     ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Distributed loops must have identical iteration ranges  
   - No interleaved access between distributed loops  

Use temporary variable:
Use temp variable to store result to avoid complex memory access

Replace multi-way conditionals with arithmetic masking, ensuring uniform execution paths across iterations.

    // Original
    if (cond1)  
        x = value_A;  
    else if (cond2)  
        x = value_B;  
    else  
        x = value_C;
    // Optimized  
    x = cond1 * value_A + (cond2 && !cond1) * value_B + (!cond1 && !cond2) * value_C;  
     ```

Let’s go step-by-step.  

---

**1. Compilation errors in optimized code**  
The optimized code declares variable-length arrays (VLAs) `de[half_len]` and `mask[half_len]`.  
While this is valid in C99, it may cause stack overflow if `half_len` is large, but it should compile without errors in a C99-compliant compiler.  
Given the compiler feedback provided, the code compiled successfully (no errors), so we proceed.

---

**2. Semantic comparison with simple test cases**  

The original code:  
- `j` starts at `-1` each outer iteration.  
- For each `i` in `0..half_len-1`, `j` increments by 1, then writes `a[j] = b[i] + d[i]*e[i]`.  
- If `c[i] > 0`, `j` increments again and writes `a[j] = c[i] + d[i]*e[i]`.  
- So `a` is filled sequentially, but the number of writes per `i` is either 1 or 2 depending on `c[i]`.  

The optimized code:  
- Phase 1 precomputes `de[i] = d[i]*e[i]` and `mask[i] = (c[i] > 0)`.  
- Phase 2 replicates the original loop’s logic but uses precomputed `de[i]` and `mask[i]`.  

This is **semantically identical** because:  
- `d[i]*e[i]` is computed before any writes to `a` in Phase 2, same as original.  
- `mask[i]` is computed from `c[i]` before Phase 2, but `c[i]` is never modified, so no dependency issue.  
- The order of writes to `a` is exactly the same as original (sequential, same `j` increments).  

Unit test passed, so semantics are correct.

---

**3. Vectorization analysis**  

Compiler feedback says:  
- First loop (Phase 1) is vectorized (width 4, interleaved count 2).  
- Second loop (Phase 2) is **not vectorized** because:  
  1. `j` is a scalar that increments conditionally, so the store `a[j]` has a variable index that depends on previous iterations (loop-carried dependency on `j`).  
  2. The compiler cannot prove `a[j]` accesses don’t overlap with `b`, `c`, `d`, `e`, etc., but the main blocker is the unknown, data-dependent index `j`.  

This is a **true dependency** preventing vectorization of Phase 2.  

---

**4. Optimization opportunity**  

The Phase 2 loop cannot be vectorized in its current form because `j` is data-dependent on `c[i] > 0`.  
However, we can transform the loop to compute `j` values first (prefix sum of `1 + mask[i]`), then scatter stores in a vectorized manner.  

But the compiler feedback indicates the current optimized code still has a non-vectorizable loop, so **there is optimization opportunity** (Phase 2 can be restructured for vectorization).  

---

**5. Conclusion**  

Since Phase 2 is not vectorized and there is a clear way to improve it (e.g., compute write indices first, then use gather/scatter or separate conditional/non-conditional writes), the code is **not fully optimized**.  

---

**Possible Transformations**  
Loop Splitting:  
**Type 1 - Instruction Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Multiple independent statements exist within the loop body  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i) { 
       A[i] = ...;  // Independent group 1
       B[i] = ...;  // Independent group 2 
     }
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 1  
   for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 2  
   ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Total iteration count remains identical  
   - Memory access order preserved across all arrays  

**Type 2 - Iteration Range Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Loop contains phase-dependent patterns (e.g., mid-point update)  
   - Vectorization blocked by conditional value changes  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i=0; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   // Phase 1: i=0~mid (pre-update)  
   for(i=0; i<mid; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   // Mid-point update  
   a[mid] = a[mid] + b[mid];  
   // Phase 2: i=mid+1~end (post-update)  
   for(i=mid+1; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   ```  
   *Validation Checklist*:  
   - Split boundaries cover original iteration range  
   - No overlapping writes between split segments  

Loop Reordering and Instruction Reordering:
**Application Rules**:  
   *Priority Patterns*:  
   - Move loop-invariant computations upward  
     ```c
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = x + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = x + y;
     ```  
   - Solve loop dependency
    ```c
    // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     ```
   - Cluster memory operations with similar addresses 

Loop Distribution:
**When to Apply**:  
   - Multiple independent write targets in loop body  
   - Example:  
     ```c
     // Original
     for(i) {
       A[i] = ...;  // Writes to A
       B[i] = ...;  // Writes to B (independent)
     }
     
     // Distributed
     for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  
     for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  
     ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Distributed loops must have identical iteration ranges  
   - No interleaved access between distributed loops

    else if (cond2)

    else  
        x = value_C;
    // Optimized  
    x = cond1 * value_A + (cond2 && !cond1) * value_B + (!cond1 && !cond2) * value_C;

```c
void s123_opt(int iterations, int LEN_1D, float* a, float* b, float* c, float* d, float* e)
{
    int half_len = LEN_1D / 2;
    
    for (int nl = 0; nl < iterations; nl++) {
        // Phase 1: Compute all d[i]*e[i] and condition masks
        float de[half_len];
        int mask[half_len];
        for (int i = 0; i < half_len; i++) {
            de[i] = d[i] * e[i];
            mask[i] = c[i] > (float)0.;
        }
        
        // Phase 2: Compute write positions using prefix sum
        int write_pos[half_len + 1];
        write_pos[0] = 0;
        for (int i = 0; i < half_len; i++) {
            write_pos[i + 1] = write_pos[i] + 1 + mask[i];
        }
        
        // Phase 3: Write non-conditional results (vectorizable)
        for (int i = 0; i < half_len; i++) {
            int pos = write_pos[i];
            a[pos] = b[i] + de[i];
        }
        
        // Phase 4: Write conditional results (vectorizable)
        for (int i = 0; i < half_len; i++) {
            if (mask[i]) {
                int pos = write_pos[i] + 1;
                a[pos] = c[i] + de[i];
            }
        }
    }
}

**1. Compilation errors in the optimized code**  
The optimized code uses variable-length arrays (VLAs) `float de[half_len]` and `int mask[half_len]`, which are not standard in C++ (though allowed in C99). The compiler feedback suggests it compiled (since we see vectorization remarks), so no compilation errors.

**2. Semantic comparison**  

**Source code logic:**  
- `j` starts at `-1`.  
- For each `i` from `0` to `LEN_1D/2 - 1`:  
  1. `j++` → `j = i*2` at start of iteration if all previous `c[i] > 0`.  
  2. `a[j] = b[i] + d[i] * e[i]`.  
  3. If `c[i] > 0`, `j++` then `a[j] = c[i] + d[i] * e[i]`.  

So `a` is filled with either 1 or 2 elements per `i`, depending on `c[i] > 0`. The total number of writes to `a` is `half_len + count(c[i] > 0)` per outer iteration.  

**Optimized code logic:**  
- Phase 1: compute `de[i]` and `mask[i]` (1 if `c[i] > 0`).  
- Phase 2: prefix sum to compute write positions: `write_pos[i]` = total writes before index `i`.  
- Phase 3: write `a[write_pos[i]] = b[i] + de[i]` for all `i`.  
- Phase 4: if `mask[i]`, write `a[write_pos[i] + 1] = c[i] + de[i]`.  

This matches the source code’s write pattern exactly:  
- `write_pos[i]` corresponds to `j` after incrementing once at start of iteration `i`.  
- `write_pos[i] + 1` corresponds to `j` after second increment when `c[i] > 0`.  

**Unit test says PASS**, so semantics match.  

✅ **Semantics correct.**

- Phase 3 and Phase 4 not vectorized because `a[pos]` has variable index `pos` that the compiler cannot prove as contiguous or without aliasing issues.  

**Why Phase 3/4 not vectorized:**

- Compiler cannot determine memory access pattern or bounds for `a[pos]`.  

**Optimization opportunity:**  
The main bottleneck is the **scatter** store in Phase 3/4. Scatter stores are supported in AVX2/AVX-512, but the compiler may not auto-vectorize here due to complexity.
